# MS.990 Parallel Edition: Canvases 95–124

## Page 124 — IIIF Canvas #95

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0095.JP2` (5523 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0095.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0095.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Remedies for cooling eye pain, swollen eyes (using a red toad-frog), crab lice in the eyebrows (with mercury salve or goldsmith's gilding water), and a full-color portrait illustrating unnatural redness of the eyes.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Portrait of a man with unnatural redness of the eyes** (`#xywh=2420,1920,2530,2940`): Bust portrait in watercolor of a mustachioed man wearing a yellow cap with red plume and a green coat with pink collar, depicting bloodshot, inflamed sclerae to illustrate 'unnatural redness of the eyes' (Von Vnnatirlichen Röde der augen).

## Page 126 — IIIF Canvas #97

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0097.JP2` (5567 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0097.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0097.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Ophthalmological remedies for bloodshot eyes / subconjunctival hemorrhage ('underloffenes Bluett in den augen'), featuring a portrait of a nun with reddened eyes, followed by an etiology of conjunctival bleeding from trauma or plethora, an initial plaster, and various poultices and eye waters.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Portrait of a nun with bloodshot eyes** (`#xywh=230,660,2230,2700`): Front-facing bust illustration in ink and watercolor of a nun wearing a black veil, white wimple, and brown habit, with visibly reddened, bloodshot eyes illustrating subconjunctival hemorrhage.

### Left Column

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | **[Rubric] Von under Loffnen Bluett in den** | Of bloodshot [subconjunctival] blood in the *— Note: Red-ink rubric heading.* |
| 2 | **[Rubric] augen** | eyes. *— Note: Red flourish after 'augen'; '.S.' written in brown ink to the right above the portrait.* |
| 3 | Das Coagulierte oder geruñe Bluett. in dn | The coagulated or clotted blood in the *— Note: 'geruñe' = geronnene; 'dn' = den.* |
| 4 | augen. auf dem Euseristen hidlein der | eyes, upon the outermost membrane of the *— Note: 'hidlein' = Häutlein.* |
| 5 | Coniunctiua. khumbt mehrste mall Von dn | conjunctiva, comes most often from |
| 6 | schlagen. werffen. stosse, fallen. ṽ dergleichen. | blows, throwing, knocks, falling, and the like. *— Note: 'ṽ' = und.* |

| 18 | nehmen. | [continued from previous line] |

| 20 | **[Rubric] Legen** | [continued from previous line] |

| 23 | dislein vber dis. | cloth over this. *— Note: 'dislein' = Tüchlein/Pölsterlein.* |

## Page 127 — IIIF Canvas #98

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0098.JP2` (5597 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0098.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0098.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Remedies and surgical extraction techniques for foreign bodies in the eye (dust, steel splinters, stones), including an illustration of three blunt-ended/knobbed eye probes/needles, followed by eye waters and a section on bee, wasp, and hornet stings in the eye.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Three eye probes / blunt-ended needles** (`#xywh=3350,1480,1610,680`): Three horizontal yellow-tinted ink-and-wash drawings of smooth, blunt-ended or knobbed surgical probes/needles ('glates khnäpfel') used to sweep under the eyelid to remove foreign bodies.

## Page 128 — IIIF Canvas #99

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0099.JP2` (5558 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0099.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0099.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Remedies for hornet/wasp stings in the eye, eyes damaged by smoke, dust, and wind (waters and plasters), and a section on spongy/wild flesh ('Schwamen und Willtn fleiß') growing on the eye, with warm bread-crumb and emollient poultice treatments.

## Page 129 — IIIF Canvas #100

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0100.JP2` (5576 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0100.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0100.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Page 129 (verso) concludes a treatment for wild/fungous eye flesh with two powder recipes (using burnt copper, tutty, haematite, and litharge), then describes the surgical excision ('handt Chur') of ocular growths/pterygium using a curved needle threaded with silk, illustrated by a portrait of a veiled patient with excised ocular masses and two surgical needles below, followed by a recipe for a comfrey-root and egg-white dressing.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**.F.** (`#xywh=3250,280,2000,3420`): Watercolor illustration of a female patient's head in a red headscarf/veil, showing two circular flesh-colored ocular growths/excisions hanging from the eyes with red silk sutures and blood streaks.

**Surgical needles** (`#xywh=3250,3750,2050,1000`): Two curved surgical needles (one threaded with red silk) drawn below the patient portrait.

### Left Column

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | 129 | 129 *— Note: Original page number in upper-left boxed corner.* |
| 2 | in ain häffel gemischt worden. v̈ in der | mixed in a small pot, and in the *— Note: Continuation from p. 128; 'v̈' abbreviates 'und'.* |
| 3 | Milch er wormet. V̈ auf das wilde flaisch | milk warmed, and upon the wild flesh *— Note: 'wildes Fleisch' = proud/fungous flesh.* |
| 4 | alle tag 3 mall ge tröpffelt. V̈ auf gelegt. | dripped 3 times every day, and laid upon [it]. |

## Page 130 — IIIF Canvas #101

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0101.JP2` (5620 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0101.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0101.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Page 130 (recto) concludes remarks on surgical complications of ocular growths, then treats poor vision after sunrise ('Von denen die nach aufgang der Sohn übel oder Wenig sehen', hemeralopia) with an electuary and distilled eye water, followed by a salve and head-wash, and finally treats night blindness ('Von denen die nach Undergang der Sohnen wenig oder gar nicht Sehen', nyctalopia) with a goat's-liver powder.

## Page 131 — IIIF Canvas #102

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0102.JP2` (5550 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0102.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0102.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Page 131 (verso) continues remedies for night blindness ('nach Undergang der Sohn nit Sehen'), including a distilled eye water, a poultice ('Umbschlag'), a head-wash, and goat's-liver/gall drops; then in the right column introduces 'Von Schwindung der Augen' (atrophy/wasting of the eyes due to liver heat and blocked tear glands), with a head-wash and a marshmallow-and-rose-syrup eye water.

## Page 132 — IIIF Canvas #103

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0103.JP2` (5625 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0103.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0103.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Recto page 132 of Wellcome Collection MS.990, divided into two columns by a vertical red rule. Contains the conclusion of an ophthalmic water recipe from the preceding page, an alternative eye-water recipe, and a treatise with multiple therapeutic and topical recipes for trichiasis ('Von den Vnrechten harn der augen Liden', i.e., ingrown or misdirected eyelashes irritating the cornea), including purgative powders, bloodletting, cupping, vesicatories, epilation, and depilatory/follicle-sealing powders.

### Left Column

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | hernach in NB: D: und des tags 2 a 3 | ...afterwards in [NB: D:], and applied a little into the eyes 2 to 3 times a day. *— Note: 17th-century German Kurrentschrift with Latin medical terms (tunica Cornea, Portulaca, Tragant, Melancolischen, continüiren) in humanistic script. Red-ink rubrics demarcate the new recipe and chapter heading. The first two lines continue a recipe from the facing verso (p. 131). The number '49' at the bottom left appears to be a section or folio/quire reference. Standard Kurrent abbreviations include 'u' for 'und', 'aa.' for 'ana', and apothecary weight symbols (℥, ß).* |
| 2 | mall ein wenig in die augen gethan | Another Water in this Condition. |
| 3 | **[Rubric] Ein anders Waßer in disen Zuestandt.** | Take: Purslane, 2 oz.; blue violet [and] ground-ivy juice, 2 oz.; barley yeast [or meal], sugar candy, of each 1.5 oz.; purslane seed, tragacanth, white lily root, of each 1 oz.; rose juice, 1.5 oz.; leaves [?], of each 0.5 oz.; pound everything together and use as was said for the previous water. |
| 4 | ℞. Portulaca. ℥ij. Plau Viol. Geündsafft. ℥ij. | Of the Misdirected Hairs of the Eyelids, which Injure and Offend the Eyeball. |
| 5 | Gersten Höhf. Zückher Candl. aa. ℥iß. portulaca | This condition is a harsh and grievous evil, which deprives countless people of their sight; for these little hairs turn/move themselves a little [inward], as if continuing without ceasing day and night, swelling and stiffening, so that the eye is brought to a continual watering, such that afterwards the tunica cornea becomes thick and dark from the incessant pokes, and is ruined in such a way that the patient must lose his sight entirely, and even if some slight glimmer of vision is preserved, nothing worse can befall such eyes. |
| 6 | samen. Tragant. weis Lilgen wurzl. aa. ℥j. | The cause of this condition is a coarse melancholic blood, which follows the moisture of the eyes, and through the pores of the eyes, with its sweating out, finally settles itself commonly on the upper eyelids, and pricks into the eyes and presses upon them from above, so that the hair grows somewhat crooked/inward. Likewise it comes from the coarse superfluous humors, which settle themselves in a glassy/glistening form on the outer parts of those same eyelids, so that from the inflammation/outbreak they only grow out toward the eyeball. |

## Page 133 — IIIF Canvas #104

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0104.JP2` (5562 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0104.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0104.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Early New High German medical recipes (Wellcome MS.990, p. 133, verso) describing treatment of inward-turning eyelashes ('böse Haare der Augen') by means of corrosive pastes ('Corrosiff') applied to the eyelids while the patient lies stretched out on a bench, followed by three additional corrosive recipes and a healing eyewash ('Wasser nach dem Corosiff zur Haillung').

### Left Column

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | **[Rubric] 133** | 133. The second cure is done by the corrosive and cauterization. Rub a piece of salmon flesh with 4 sinews, cut several [slits] toward the light from the front, make a through-draft like a brick or furrow thick, and lay it across the board, 2 spans long; let the patient lie on the bench straight lengthwise, stretched out, so that he lies with his neck on the folded cloth, and the head hangs over what [remains of] the cushion, and thus all extremities remain stretched out. Afterwards rub a fine, clear, fat [hen's fat / tallow?]; lift his eyelids fully up, fill his eyes therein completely full, so that the whole eyeball [is covered] or stands right, and the corrosive cannot come to the eye. Yet make it [so] that the hair does not come into the eye, but is turned completely outwards and remains so; afterwards apply the following corrosive, which you must have in readiness over a little ember. Afterwards with a little wooden/felt tube, several fine [drops] spattered on, nicely warm and fairly thick; it will begin to break [the skin] around the eyes, so let him lie thus for a while or half an hour on the cushion; afterwards wipe the corrosive cleanly away again with a little sponge or soft cloth, so that nothing remains hanging on the eyelids, nor anything thereof melts into the eye [where] you must scratch the handful away. When everything is cleanly washed/wiped away, rub the [fat] out of the eye again, and use the water which I describe [for] the healing of the corrosive. *— Note: Page number '133' boxed in red ink at upper left; red ruling lines frame the text block and divide the two columns. Standard 17th-century Kurrentschrift with rubricated headings in red ink. Alchemical glyphs used: ℞ (recipe), ⊕ (vitriol/copperas or salt), 🜔 (sal/salt, circle with horizontal bar), ☉ (circle with center dot), ✚ (acetum/vinegar), △ (ignis/fire), ▽ (aqua/water), ℥ (ounce). Several minor deletions ('apffel', 'der', 'wein') and one supralinear/inline addition ('stett').* |

Make a powder, mix, and put into a clean glazed little pot. Pour thereon good strong vinegar (✚) [del: wine] full or more, so that it becomes like a thin paste. ## Page 134 — IIIF Canvas #105

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0105.JP2` (5620 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0105.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0105.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Recto folio (p. 134) in a two-column layout detailing the third surgical cure for trichiasis ('Die drite Chur ist. die har sambt den fleisch Hinwegh Zue Schneiden'), involving suturing and excising the eyelid margin, followed by post-operative poultice/plaster care using egg white and a mineral/gold-marked ingredient, a warning to operate while the patient is fasting, and a powder recipe for proud flesh ('Ein Puluer Zum überigen fleisch'). The right column features a large watercolor anatomical/surgical illustration of a face with blood dripping from both eyes and a curved needle/lancet at the left eye.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Surgical illustration of eyelid incision and bleeding** (`#xywh=2450,1440,2150,2880`): Watercolor portrait of a young person's head with shoulder-length curly brown hair, facing forward. Vertical red streaks of blood drip from both lower eyelids down the cheeks. A dark curved surgical needle or lancet is positioned at the upper margin of the patient's left eye (viewer's right), illustrating the suturing and excision of the eyelid.

### Left Column

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | vñ geht gern über, vñ reibt es woll vnder ein ander | ...and easily overflows, and rub it well together; then wipe the eye-region for him twice every day into the eyes, once the hairs have already been corroded away; it heals very nicely. *— Note: Early New High German Kurrent script with standard nasal bar abbreviations (vñ for vnd). A large rubbed/water-stained patch obscures parts of lines 8–11 in the left column. Rubricated headings are written in red ink. The symbol ☉ (Sol) in line 37 appears as a circle with a central dot.* |
| 2 | den wisch Ihme dz gebites alle tag 2 mall in | [Rubric:] The third cure is to cut away the hairs together with the flesh. |
| 3 | die augen. wan die har schon abgeäzt sein. es | When everything is prepared for this, seat the patient on a chair, bind him in all [respects ...] as is shown in the figure [...], so that he does not reach in between with his hands or untie himself. Thereafter take a surgical suture needle (Heftnadel) threaded with a red [...] silk thread; thereafter take a suture cannula (Heftröhrlein), gently lift the eyelid, and pierce it with the suture needle. Pull the thread through and cut it off so that it hangs a finger's length at both ends, as the following figure clearly shows you. Such piercing should be done 3 times on each eyelid, or 4 times; then grasp all 3 or 4 threads in one hand and draw the eyelid evenly forward. Hold it thus evenly elevated, and with the other hand take the scissors and cut evenly behind the thread from one corner to the other, and take great care that you do not cut outside the corner of the eye-angles and leave no hair-stump standing; and the eyelid should be cut away evenly up to the width of 2 knife-backs, so that the hair roots are removed everywhere, and it is properly done thereafter. |
| 4 | hailet gar schön. | [Rubric:] Now follows the cure and healing of the cut eyes. |

## Page 135 — IIIF Canvas #106

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0106.JP2` (5616 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0106.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0106.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Conclusion of a preceding wound-healing treatment, followed by a section on 'flashes and fire-flames of the eyes' (photopsia / early cataract or black star) attributed to overloaded ocular humors and congealed blood, treated with purgation, vesicatories behind the ears (illustrated), herbal decoctions, a head wash, distilled eye water, and an eye plaster.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Vesicatoria behind the ears** (`#xywh=3280,580,2180,2140`): Watercolor illustration of the back of a man's curly brown head showing two rectangular white blistering plasters (vesicatoria) applied behind the left and right ears, bounded below by a red horizontal rule.

## Page 136 — IIIF Canvas #107

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0107.JP2` (5656 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0107.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0107.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Continuation of the eye plaster recipe and an eye ointment for the preceding condition, followed by a detailed surgical treatise on fused eyelids ('Zusamen gewachsne augen' / ankyloblepharon), describing how to separate the upper and lower eyelids with a suture needle, silk thread, knife, and scissors, illustrated with a portrait of a woman with closed eyes, and post-operative dressing with charpie.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Closed eyelids of a woman** (`#xywh=2460,1970,2460,2710`): Watercolor portrait of a woman's face with closed eyes, dark curly hair adorned with red ribbons/bows, and a gold band and red bead necklace, illustrating fused/closed eyelids above a red horizontal dividing rule.

## Page 137 — IIIF Canvas #108

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0108.JP2` (5618 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0108.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0108.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Treatise on 'growths of the eyes' ('Von den Gewächßen der augen'), describing benign/fatty eyelid tumors and their surgical excision using a suture thread, small knife, and scissors (illustrated with a man's face showing bleeding eyelid tumors pierced by threads), followed by post-operative dressing with egg white, water, and tow, and a wound-healing ointment recipe ('Ain guettes Salbel Zue den geschnidtenen augen gewächß').

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Excision of eyelid growths** (`#xywh=3140,300,2380,2800`): Watercolor portrait of a mustachioed and goateed man's face with two large round reddish growths protruding from both eyelids, each pierced by suture threads and dripping blood down his cheeks, bounded below by a red-orange horizontal rule.

## Page 138 — IIIF Canvas #109

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0109.JP2` (5685 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0109.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0109.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Recto page 138 in two columns: 'Von Geschwerlein der augen gersten oder habern genand' (On small ulcers/styes of the eyes, called barleycorns or oats). Describes styes/chalazia on the upper and lower eyelids, accompanied by a large watercolor portrait of a man's face with stylized black geometric facial hair and inflamed/swollen eyelids. Followed by causes (salted thick blood and viscous phlegm), a poultice ('die Erste Khur. ain Vmbschlag'), an ointment ('Ain andere Guete Salben'), two plasters ('Ain Guettes Pflaster dargüe', 'Ain Anders'), and instructions for surgical incision if salves and plasters fail.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Eye Stye / Hordeolum Portrait** (`#xywh=180,1580,2200,2770`): Watercolor portrait of a man with brown curly hair, stylized black geometric moustache and goatee patches, and swollen reddish upper eyelids illustrating eye styes ('gersten oder habern').

## Page 139 — IIIF Canvas #110

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0110.JP2` (5584 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0110.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0110.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Verso page 139 in two columns: Continuation of surgical procedure for eye styes (threading with a curved needle and silk thread, incising lengthwise, pressing out fluid, dressing with egg white). Followed by a new chapter: 'Von den Waser Galln der augen. oder gewachs der augen' (On water-galls or growths of the eyes / Atheromata), with a recipe for 'Ein Sehr guette Confecto Salben' containing killed mercury, surgical excision instructions, a watercolor portrait ('Figur der Waser Gall') showing bilateral swollen eyelid cysts, and post-operative dressing with digestive ointment and mercury precipitate.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Figur der Waser Gall** (`#xywh=2820,3350,2460,2570`): Watercolor portrait of a man with brown curly hair, thin moustache and goatee, displaying two large round fluid-filled swellings ('water galls' / atheromata) on both upper eyelids.

## Page 140 — IIIF Canvas #111

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0111.JP2` (5736 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0111.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0111.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Recto page 140 in two columns: Completion of the water-gall healing instructions, followed by a new chapter: 'Von den Zittern vnd Vippern der augen Glidern' (On trembling and twitching of the eyelids). Discusses intermittent, continuous, hereditary, and convulsive eyelid twitching and their humoral/windy causes, followed by internal and external remedies: two herbal drinks ('Ain Guets Tranckh', 'Ain anders Tranckh'), a head wash ('Ain guete haubt Waschung'), two eye waters ('Ain Guetes Waser', 'Ain anders Waser'), an eyelid salve ('Ain guets Sälbel'), a snuff powder ('Ain guets Schnupf Puluor'), and dietary/regimen warnings (avoiding solitude, late suppers, cold/moist foods, and fog).

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0112.JP2` (5673 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0112.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0112.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Remedies and dietary regimen for burned and injured eyes, including a verbena-water and quince-seed mucilage poultice, poppy-seed and olive-oil salve for raw flesh, houseleek and terra sigillata eye drops, and a breast-milk and rose-water wash.

## Page 142 — IIIF Canvas #113

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0113.JP2` (5542 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0113.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0113.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Full-page coloured illustration of a Franciscan apothecary's eye-remedy dispensary framed by an architectural arch, showing five shelves of labelled eye medicines (pills, powders, ointments, waters, confections) and a friar handing a bottle to a blindfolded patient.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Apothecary Eye Dispensary** (`#xywh=190,110,4610,5510`): Full-page coloured pen-and-ink wash illustration framed by two blue marbled columns and a yellow-and-grey stone arch. Five yellow shelves hold rows of apothecary vessels under hanging signs: Augen Pilule (11 red cylindrical jars), Augen Puluer (10 red jars), Augen salben (8 blue-and-white ceramic albarelli), Augen waser (7 square glass bottles), and Augen Confect (12 red jars). Below, a tonsured Franciscan friar in a brown habit with a rosary hands a small bottle to a blindfolded gentleman holding a hat and walking stick, while two more blindfolded patients with sticks enter through a doorway on the left. A framed chart hangs on the back wall and a table on the right holds apothecary vessels.

### Full-Width Text

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | 142 | 142 *— Note: Boxed page number at upper right.* |
| 2 | Augen Pilule | Eye Pills *— Note: Hanging sign on top shelf of illustration.* |
| 3 | Augen Puluer | Eye Powders *— Note: Hanging sign on second shelf of illustration.* |
| 4 | Augen salben | Eye Ointments *— Note: Hanging sign on third shelf of illustration.* |
| 5 | Augen waser | Eye Waters *— Note: Hanging sign on fourth shelf of illustration.* |
| 6 | Augen Confect | Eye Confections *— Note: Hanging sign on fifth shelf of illustration.* |

Introduction to the 'Apothecary of the Poor' for ocular remedies, followed by an illustration of two pillboxes and nine recipes for ophthalmic and cephalic purgative pills (pilulae lucis maiores, cochiarum, sine quibus esse nolo, aurearum, de succino Cratonis, etc.).

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Two Pillboxes** (`#xywh=830,2040,2280,670`): Coloured pen-and-ink wash illustration on a yellow background band showing two round ribbed wooden or earthenware pillboxes filled with dark spherical pills.

## Page 144 — IIIF Canvas #115

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0115.JP2` (5604 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0115.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0115.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Two-column page containing recipes for purging pills (especially for head and rheumy eye pains, including 'ascarisch Pillule'), laxative powders, an illustration of five colored apothecary flasks for eye waters, and a recipe for red and running eyes.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Five Eye-Water Flasks** (`#xywh=2560,4190,2110,720`): Row of five rectangular/octagonal colored apothecary bottles or flasks with stoppers (reddish-purple, blue, pink, tan, dark blue-green) standing on a brown shelf beneath the heading for various eye waters.

## Page 145 — IIIF Canvas #116

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0116.JP2` (5591 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0116.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0116.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

## Page 147 — IIIF Canvas #118

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0118.JP2` (5602 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0118.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0118.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Verso folio (p. 147) in two columns containing ophthalmic recipes: a cataplasm for barleycorns (styes) on the eye, a wondrous eye-water for all fluxes and strengthening vision distilled per alembicum in a Balneum Mariae, additional eye-waters (including pike gall, celandine, valerian, eyebright), remedies for 'flies' before the eyes, incipient cataracts (starn), hot eye fluxes, and films/spots (fell und maill).

## Page 148 — IIIF Canvas #119

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0119.JP2` (5528 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0119.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0119.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Recto folio (p. 148) in two columns continuing the celandine recipe from p. 147, followed by remedies for eye fluxes and pain-stilling, eye spots (with gum sagapenum), films and blemishes (buck's gall, houseleek, whey, goose fat, cinquefoil), dangerous eye ulcers (tutia, pearls, sarcocolla), and in the right column a precious water for eye ulcers, treatment for itching/moisture of the eyes, and remedies for preventing or dispersing blue/bruised eyes.

## Page 149 — IIIF Canvas #120

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0120.JP2` (5595 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0120.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0120.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Verso folio (p. 149) in two columns titled 'Von Underschiedlichen augen Sälblen' (On various eye ointments), featuring a watercolor illustration of four apothecary ointment jars on a shelf at the top of the left column. Recipes cover ointments for heated/inflamed eye pains (tutia, coral, Armenian bole, white lead, fresh unsalted butter, hematite, pearls) and in the right column ointments for eye fistulas (with copaiba balsam), pain-stilling (quince seeds, roasted apples, sugar of lead), eye ulcers, inflammation/pustules, and a cataplasm for eye inflammation.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Four Apothecary Ointment Jars** (`#xywh=830,920,2270,740`): Watercolor illustration of four apothecary jars/pots arranged in a row on a brown shelf: from left to right, a white/grey bulbous jar, a reddish-brown jar, a yellow-ochre lidded jar, and a pinkish-red handled pitcher.

## Page 150 — IIIF Canvas #121

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0121.JP2` (5608 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0121.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0121.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Treatises on ocular films, spots, and blemishes (fell, fleckhen, maill der augen), especially for the poor and elderly. Includes dietary and humoral regimen (avoiding sadness, weeping, smoke, bloodletting, cold, wind, dust, pond fish, pork, dairy, waterfowl, and damp air), internal remedies (medicinal salt, herbal decoctions, purging pills), and external applications (ant-oil distillation, celandine water, gold/alumen, honey, and egg-yolk oil). Illustrated with a watercolor of a human eye showing a corneal opacity.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Fig. 1** (`#xywh=640,1130,850,530`): Watercolor drawing in a rectangular ink frame depicting a human eye with a brown iris and a white spot/opacity (fell/fleck) on the cornea/pupil.

## Page 151 — IIIF Canvas #122

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0122.JP2` (5577 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0122.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0122.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Collection of ophthalmic waters, powders (trisenet), and collyria for eye films and blemishes. Includes a complex eye water with pike and sheep gall, a strengthening digestive powder (trisenet), a tutia-and-malmsey collyrium aged in the sun, a sky-blue copper/vitriol water, sharp eye waters, a ripe strawberry fermentation remedy, a candle-soot insufflation, and an astrological/sympathetic dandelion root amulet dug at St. Bartholomew's Day.

## Page 152 — IIIF Canvas #123

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0123.JP2` (5695 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0123.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0123.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Remedies for eye soreness/corners and ocular blisters or pustules (Bladtern in augen). Includes fennel-herb wine, purgative head pills with grayling fat (äsch schmalz), leaf-on-anvil dew, peeled beans in wine, red mugwort root worn on the arm, hard-boiled eggs in vinegar, and ginger with egg white. Dominated by a large central watercolor scene of a bearded monk surgeon operating on the eye of a reclining gentleman.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Fig. 2** (`#xywh=430,2200,4250,3850`): Full-width watercolor scene depicting a bearded friar/monk surgeon in a brown habit performing an eye operation with a small instrument on a male patient in a blue coat and red breeches lying on a wooden bench/bed. To the left is an apothecary shelf with red jars, colored glass bottles, and a table with flasks; behind is an open wooden door and a leaded-glass window on the right.

## Page 153 — IIIF Canvas #124

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0124.JP2` (5624 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0124.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0124.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Page 153 (verso), two columns. Left column features three watercolor/ink illustrations of ophthalmic instruments (two round-headed/sponge-tipped probes and one blue-grey double-ended probe with two eyed/ringed instruments below), followed by recipes for eyes struck or bruised, 'wild flesh' (pterygium) in the eyes, and blisters/pustules forming films. Right column continues the blister/film remedy, then gives a compound eye powder ('Puluer') for failing sight or brain weakness, a remedy for films ('fell der augen'), and a hen-fat and honey ointment for spots/maculae in the eye.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Ophthalmic probes and specula/applicators** (`#xywh=850,440,2340,2170`): Three horizontal instrument drawings in the upper left column: (1) a brown rod with a stippled, round sponge- or brush-like head at the left end; (2) a brown rod with a similar stippled round head at the right end; (3) a blue-grey double-ended probe/cannula with two small blue-grey eyed/ringed instruments (possibly eye specula or trocars/guards) drawn directly beneath it.
